<insert>85.221</insert>

<summary>This is the title page of a 1709 theosophical and alchemical treatise titled "Theosophical Hall of Wonders" or "Theater," attributed to the pseudonym Theophilus Philaletha. It promises to reveal the spiritual essence of all things, particularly the sacraments of bread and wine, through the metaphorical marriage of "Queen Juno" and "King Magniphosauri."</summary>

<keywords>Theosophy, Alchemy, Eucharist, Covenant of God, Esotericism, Theophilus Philaletha, 18th-century printing, Mysticism</keywords>

It is typical of the era to begin a mystical or theological treatise with a solemn invocation to the Divine, establishing that the knowledge contained within is dedicated to the glory of God rather than personal gain.</meta>

# To the Kind Reader

-> intended to include these few words <-
-> alongside wishes for all things good <-
-> as an exhortation and a warning <-
-> the author. <-

# The Author.

The sons of Aaron brought strange fire <note>original: "frembd Feuer" — referring to the biblical account of Nadab and Abihu offering unauthorized fire in the Tabernacle.</note> before the LORD; then fire came forth from him and consumed them, so that they died (Leviticus 10:1 <note>original: "3. Mos. 10. pr." — the "Third Book of Moses" is the traditional German name for Leviticus.</note>). Therefore, "Touch me not" (John 20:17). If you do not bring love and earnestness, but rather a false intention, take care that the darkness does not blind your eyes (1 John 2:10–11) and that you do not perish in your wickedness before the Spirit of the LORD (Acts 5:5, 10 <note>original: "Actor. 5" — a common abbreviation for the Acts of the Apostles.</note>). Also, do not think to touch me in any way out of envy; for if any man should rise up against me to pursue me and to seek my soul, then my soul shall be bound in the bundle of the living <note>original: "Bündlein der Lebendigen" — a metaphor for divine protection and eternal life.</note> with the LORD my God: but the soul of my enemies and adversaries shall be hurled away with the magical sling <note>original: "magische Schleuder" — a mystical adaptation of the biblical "sling" mentioned in 1 Samuel; here implying a supernatural defense against critics.</note> of the LORD of Hosts <note>original: "HErrn Zebaoth" — Lord of Sabaoth, a title for God as a divine warrior.</note> (1 Samuel 25:29).

<summary>In this preface, the author issues a stern spiritual warning to his readers, using biblical examples to demand respect and pure intentions when approaching his work. He claims divine protection for himself while threatening critics or ill-intentioned readers with a "magical" spiritual defense.</summary>

<keywords>preface, warning, Aaron's sons, strange fire, bundle of the living, magical sling, Lord of Hosts, spiritual protection, envy, Leviticus</keywords>

<note>original Hebrew: "כ אלהים" (K'Elohim). This phrase suggests that the echo acts as a divine reflection or a mirror of truth, much like the judgment of God described later in the text.</note>

**E**cho, or reverberation, is a wonderful play,
jest, and mystery of nature.
Its origin is marvelous, in that it is born
in the air through the spoken word,
due to an encountered obstacle <term>Gegenwurff</term> <note>literally "counter-throw"; a physical object like a mountain or wall that reflects sound back to its source</note>.
For the voice of the word resounds, 
moves into, and penetrates through 
the air as a spiritual essence; but when
its path or journey is interrupted and 
halted somewhere by a mountain, forest, 
masonry, or building, the voice turns 
around in the air, and thus an echo 
is generated from it. This is truly 
wonderful. Even more wonderful is 
the effect and art of the echo; 
for as soon as one speaks or calls 
out, it immediately gives an answer, 
and is, as it were, obedient and eager 
to comply with and serve man:
The echo also maintains a kind of 
justice, and shows no respect 
of persons; for just as one speaks or calls—

<vocab>Echo, Reverberation, Nature, Voice, Justice</vocab>

<summary>This page explores the "marvelous mystery" of the echo, describing it as a spiritual essence that reflects off physical obstacles. The author characterizes the echo as a servant of man and a symbol of impartial justice, as it returns exactly what is spoken without bias.</summary>
<keywords>Echo, natural philosophy, acoustics, spiritual essence, divine justice, reflection, metaphysics</keywords>

(8)

I charge you in the presence of God, who gives life to all things. Thus, it is God's presence that causes all things—existence, life, being, movement, preservation, and stability—as the highly enlightened instrument <note>original: "Rüstzeug," a term used to describe a person as a vessel or tool of God, frequently applied to the Apostle Paul.</note> Paul assures us here when he says: That God—by what means?—through Yah <note>original: "יה," a shortened form of the divine name Yahweh.</note> Jesus (Genesis 1:1), His eternal, spoken, self-existing <term>selbständiges</term> <note>In this theological context, this refers to Christ's nature as having existence within himself, rather than being a created or dependent being.</note> Word of Life (1 John 1:1), through whom the whole world was made; who indeed is God, yet afterwards became flesh (John 1:13, 14), and is called the only-begotten (John 1:18) and beloved Son of God (Matthew 3:17), the Life that is eternal (1 John 1:2), which was with the Father and is now also self-existing within Himself (John 5:26), the Light of the World and of Life (John 12:46, Chapter 8:12), unceasingly—for otherwise there would be no preservation of the creatures which we have before our eyes—gives life to (animates, moves, preserves, confirms) all things. These things may be heavenly, spiritual, angels, spirits, souls, or bodily, earthly, or natural; they may be called *minerals* <note>original: "mineralia"</note>, ores, stones, metal, crystal, etc.

<summary>This page explores how God's presence, acting through the "Word of Life" (Jesus Christ), provides the necessary animating force and stability for every level of existence, from celestial spirits to physical minerals.</summary>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the investigation of the life-giving Spirit described on page 9. Having established that God sustains the world through a "living Echo" or Spirit, the author now defines this Spirit using the terminology of alchemy and natural philosophy, identifying it as the "Ruach" or "Universal Spirit" that animates all matter.</meta>

10

<meta>catchword: Para-</meta>

To rightly recognize **GOD**, who is so active and effective, one must necessarily examine both more closely: first, the Spirit according to its nature, and further according to its origin and effect. Therefore, it is to be known that when **alchemy** <term>Chymie: here referring to the study of the hidden spiritual properties of matter as well as its physical composition</term> penetrates a creature to its very **center** <term>centrum</term> and explores it, it indeed finds that the origin and male seed of all things is a spiritual **homogeneous** <term>homogeneum: an essence that is uniform and consistent in its nature</term> and harmonious essence. This essence is what the **philosophers** <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers to natural philosophers and alchemists.</note> have titled with many wondrous names, such as: **Substance of Life**, **Vital Spirit**, **Light of Life**, **Balsam of Life**, **vital power** <note>original Latin: "numia vitalis"</note>, **innate heat** <note>original Latin: "Calidum nativum"</note>, **radical moisture** <note>original Latin: "humidum radicale," a term from medieval medicine and alchemy for the fundamental fluid that sustains life and prevents aging</note>, **entelechy** <note>original Greek: "Enthelechia," referring to the vital force that turns potential into reality</note>, **steel** <note>original Latin: "Chalyps"; in alchemical writing, this often refers to a "spiritual steel" or magnetic agent that attracts the influence of the stars</note>, **universal spirit**, **mercury of life** <note>original Latin: "Mercurius vitæ"</note>, etc. 

Although all of these are the same thing, namely nothing other than the **Spirit** <note>original Hebrew: "רוח" (Ruach)</note>, a pure, spiritual, and heavenly essence. When the common man looks upon it, he calls it "Heaven." It appears blue to the sight, and it consists of light, air, and a **magnetic saltpeter** <term>Magnetischen nitro: a theoretical spiritual salt believed to exist in the air and act as a vehicle for celestial energy</term>, which is the mind, sense, and sensitivity of the light and the air. **Theophrastus** <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, known as Paracelsus (1493–1541), the famous Swiss physician and alchemist.</note>

<summary>This page explores the alchemical nature of the "Spirit of Life," listing its various technical names and identifying it as the pure, blue essence of the heavens that provides sensitivity and life to the physical world.</summary>
<keywords>Spirit, alchemy, Paracelsus, Ruach, vital force, radical moisture, universal spirit, saltpeter, celestial influence</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Universal Spirit" from page 10, specifically focusing on the teachings of Paracelsus. The previous page listed various historical names for this vital essence (such as "radical moisture" and "innate heat"); this page now applies those concepts to a famous alchemical riddle regarding the soul's relationship to the body.</meta>

(11)

<meta>catchword: bens</meta>

*Paracelsus*, to whom we primarily owe **chemistry** <term>Chymie: in this era, the term encompassed both laboratory chemistry and alchemy, focusing on the hidden spiritual properties of matter</term> or natural science in Germany, has brought a riddle to light in his so-called *Book of Puzzles*: **The house is always dead, but the inhabitant lives and gives life.** <note>original Latin: "Domus est semper mortua, sed eam inhabitans vivit & vivificat."</note> That is: The house is always dead, but the inhabitant is living and makes it living as well. Over this, the **philosophers** <note>Refers to natural philosophers and alchemists of the medieval and Renaissance periods.</note> have dreadfully racked their brains to guess the riddle’s meaning, and have often brought forth such mad stuff that it could have given the largest elephant a bellyache. However, *Theophrastus* <note>The first name of Paracelsus, born Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim.</note> intends to say and indicate through this riddle how the power of creatures and bodies does not consist in the dead and perishable body, but rather that one must seek it in the wondrous **spiritual life** hidden under the shadow of the body. This life is nothing other than the aforementioned **blue substance** consisting of light, air, and a **magnet**, and is also quite fittingly and rightly called the Breath of Li-

<summary>The author explains a riddle by Paracelsus, arguing that physical bodies are "dead houses" animated by a spiritual, magnetic "blue substance" or vital breath. This passage emphasizes that the true power of nature lies in the hidden spirit rather than the visible, perishable form.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, alchemy, Vexir-Buch, riddle, spiritual life, blue substance, vital force, Theophrastus</keywords>

<meta>catchword: a</meta>

<summary>The author explains that all physical matter—from plant stalks to animal hair and even minerals—is inherently porous to allow the Universal Spirit or "Breath of Life" to flow through and animate it. He compares the necessity of this spirit to the way fire needs air to burn, suggesting that life can return even to decayed matter through this force.</summary>

§. III. Regarding the origin, please be so kind as to take a look with me into the 1st Chapter of the 1st Book of Moses <note>The Book of Genesis</note> and into the 1st Chapter of the Gospel of John; there, the origin of this **Breath of Life** will be made known to us, provided that one does not overlook the mysteries therein. > In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and God was the Word; this was in the beginning with God! All things were made through the same, and without it nothing was made that is made: in Him was life, and the life was a light for mankind.

What kind of Word that was is revealed in Genesis 1, verses 3, 6, 9, and following; and it is called **let there be** <note>original Hebrew: יהי (*Yehi*); original Latin: *fiat*</note>, from the Chaldean root **to be** <note>original Chaldean/Aramaic: היה (*Hayah*)</note> (in its full form it would be יהיה <note>meaning "He will be"</note>, from which the name of the Son of God—namely the **LORD** <note>original Hebrew: יהוה (*YHWH*)</note> and **JESUS**—originates. <meta>This page continues the linguistic and theological derivation of the creative Word. Jeremiah 23:6. Now, if through this Word—the Son begotten of the eternal God, the **LORD** <note>original Hebrew: יהוה (*YHWH*)</note>—all things that exist were created, then the **heavens** <note>original Hebrew: שמים (*shamayim*)</note>—which are subtle, volatile waters (from the Arabic root *sama*, meaning volatile or subtle, or *samawa*, to be exalted or to rise on high)—and the **earth** <note>original Hebrew: הארץ (*ha’aretz*)</note>—the fixed and dry waters located just beneath them in the depths (from the Arabic root *arada*, meaning firm, constant, or turning green; or *aradsa*, to be deep, low, or below, just as the dry earth is beneath the firmament)—from which heaven and earth were later called forth, must also have been made. This is true even though Moses remains silent regarding the name **LORD** <note>original Hebrew: יהוה (*YHWH*)</note> and only mentions His office in the beginning, namely **Created** <note>original Hebrew: ברא (*bara*)</note>.

Just as this is beyond all doubt, it is also easy for us to contemplate and grasp—through the word **LORD** <note>original Hebrew: יהוה (*YHWH*)</note> and its natural pronunciation—that the first or initial outflow of God was a breath (Wisdom of Solomon 7:25). This breath manifested in the center <note>original Latin: *in centro*</note> of the eternal expanse through a dark, cold impression. To speak in human terms, this is much like a warm vapor in a receiver <note>An alchemical term for a vessel used to collect distilled substances</note>, or a human breath against a cold [glass]... <note>The text ends mid-sentence; the catchword "Glase" indicates the next page begins with "glass."</note>

From this, the most subtle part (**masculine essence** <note>original Latin: *ens masculinum*</note>) swiftly rose and moved upon the surface or face of the **fixed and dry part** (**feminine essence** <note>original Latin: *ens fœmininum*</note>)—just as a mist might rise over the earth. Hovering over it as a **LORD** <note>original Hebrew: יהוה (*YHWH*)</note>—a spiritual, subtle, blue, penetrating, and active essence—it awakened movement and life within the dry part, much like a bird warming the eggs beneath it. <meta>This page continues the argument from the previous section, explaining how the "Breath of Life" (Ruach) acts upon matter. It focuses on the alchemical process where a "dead" or "desolate" body—one stripped of its spiritual essence—acts as a magnet to draw the vital spirit back into itself from the surrounding air.</meta>

(22)

...to restore life; the desolate body also attracts it with the greatest desire. For example: When one has driven out and separated the various spiritual <term>substances</term> <note>In alchemy, these are the volatile essences or "spirits" of a mineral that can be extracted through heat.</note> contained within vitriol or salt, and has thus made it dull and tasteless, and then the <term>caput mortuum</term> <note>original Latin: "dead head"—the inert solid residue remaining after the distillation or sublimation of a substance.</note>, or the desolate remaining earth (in which at least the matter of the element remains, which is impossible to completely separate from the <term>quartz</term> <note>Here used to describe the hard, stony, and inert physical state of matter after its "life" has been removed.</note>) is laid out openly in the air, even in a place safe from rain, then the **Breath of Life** most certainly takes up its seat there again immediately. ### §. VI.

Now reason advises us to treat somewhat of the **elements** and their properties. I must immediately—contrary to all **Peripatetic philosophy** <note>original: *peripathetischen Philosophie*; referring to the traditional Aristotelian teachings that dominated universities at the time.</note> and regardless of its current **authority**—report and establish from Holy Scripture and experience that, just as there is only one **breath of life** as an **active principle** <note>original: *thätliches principium*.</note>, so too there is only one **element** as a **passive principle** <note>original: *leidend principium*; the "receiver" of the active spirit.</note>. 

This element is namely the **the Earth** <note>original: הארץ (*ha-aretz*); the Hebrew word for Earth or Land.</note> or **dry water**, or the **crystalline mirror**, wherein the breath of life mirrors and represents itself, or casts off the **subterranean Juno** <note>In alchemical allegory, Juno often represents the air or a specific metallic essence.</note>, with whom **Magniphosaurus** <note>A unique alchemical name, likely referring to a "Great Light-Serpent" or a specific mineral spirit the author identifies.</note> falls in love.

§. VII. I am compelled here, in order to give further emphasis to my previous *theosophizing* <note>The author uses *Theosophiren* to describe his method of exploring divine mysteries through the study of nature.</note>, against my will—yet in the confident hope that it shall nonetheless remain hidden from the unworthy—to communicate several processes through which one can behold and contemplate not only the **Breath of Life** in bodily form / but also its element together with its **quartz** <note>In this context, *Quarz* likely refers to the "salt" or crystalline mineral body that remains after the spiritual essence is extracted.</note> / and a body that is—albeit according to our current state of imperfection—**Clarified**.

### Anatomy or Dissection of the Grain.

This operation and labor—if one wishes to have and enjoy the desired effect from it—must be undertaken in the spring, especially around the time of the *equinox* <note>original: *aequinoctij*; the time of year when day and night are of equal length, occurring in March and September.</note>, when day and night are equal. This is because the Magnetic Light-Air or the Breath of Life is, at that time, as it were, occupied and in a state of motion, in order to make all things sprout forth, and to make them living and renewed. Therefore, it opens all its treasures and applies its powers to this end, which make the Earth fertile by means of dew-rain and also river-water; for at this time of year, that same water is completely filled with a very subtle, volatile, and powerful *nitre* <note>original: *nitro*; in alchemy, this refers to "philosophical saltpeter," a substance believed to contain the concentrated celestial fire that fuels growth.</note>, or 🜔 <note>Alchemical symbol for Nitre/Saltpeter</note> in which Light and Air are concentrated. Hence, one should take, for example, grain—wheat, rye, barley, oats, and the like—and let it soak in a wooden tub or vessel in dew-water, rain-water, or river-water, so that the water remains several fingers high above the grain; let it stand thus for twenty-four hours, and thereafter let the remaining water [drain] through the hole, which be- <note>The text cuts off here at the catchword "un-ten," meaning "below."</note>

<summary>This page describes the "Anatomy of Grain," a practical alchemical process that must be performed during the spring equinox to harness the "Magnetic Light-Air" or universal life force. The author provides instructions for soaking various grains in water enriched by "nitre" to awaken their inner vitality.</summary>

<keywords>Alchemy, Grain, Spring Equinox, Nitre, Vitalism, Agriculture, Natural Philosophy, Saltpeter</keywords>

The **radical moisture** and **heat** fade away, and because of the lack and poverty of the **chyle**, the **blood**, and the **juices**, etc., the **nerves**, **muscles**, **lungs**, **heart**, and similar life-moving...

### §. XI.

This glorious **Revelation** <note>original: *Offenbarung*; the process of God making Himself known to humanity</note> now also leads us to the **knowledge of ourselves** <note>original: *Erkantniß unser selbst*</note> or of our condition, both inside and outside of **Paradise**. Within Paradise, indeed, we see how man was made and created in God’s **Image** <note>original: *Ebenbild*; the *Imago Dei*, or the reflection of God's nature within the human soul</note> (Genesis 1:26, 27; James 3:9). Outside of Paradise, however, we see how man is a **Devil's Mask** <note>original: *Teuffels-Larve*; a deceptive or grotesque outward appearance that hides the original divine spark behind a fallen nature</note>—namely a villain, darkness, and sinner (John 8:44; 2 Peter 2:4; 1 John 3:8). ### ❁ (93) ❁

...to imagine discontent, as the Cain <note>The author uses Cain, the first-born of Adam, as a metaphor for the first manifestation of sin or "discontent" arising from the soul.</note> of the seed of self-love <note>original: *Φιλαυτία* (*philautia*); a Greek term for excessive or selfish love of oneself.</note> or the high-esteem of himself, which had already taken a form in his outer characteristics. God, seeing this, began and spoke (Genesis 2, verse 18): "It is not good (notwithstanding that in Genesis 1, verse 31 it was stated: 'and behold! it was all very good') that the man should be by himself alone: I will make him a helper <note>original: *Gehülffe*; a companion or mate.</note> that must be suited to him." Why was that? Indeed! God saw clearly that Adam would not want to bring forth offspring magically <note>In the mystical tradition this text follows, "magical" birth refers to a spiritual, non-material reproduction Adam was supposedly capable of before his desires became earthly.</note>, because through his imagination <note>original: *Imagination*; here meaning the power of the mind to focus on and desire material things.</note> his lust had already entered into animal flesh, as no helper was found for him who corresponded to his discontent, imagination, and fancy (verse 20; observe the linguistic root <note>original: *observ. ling. rad.*; a reference to studying the root of the original Hebrew words.</note>). To break and destroy this discontent before it could take root, the LORD God caused a deep sleep to fall upon the man, through which He simultaneously prefigured the unpleasantness, bitterness, and loathsomeness of death—which had been preached to him in the Law—in order to heal the stirred and awakened lust, whose imagination was now to be satisfied through...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how believers participate in the salvation and renewal brought by Christ, specifically linking the New Testament sacraments of Baptism and the Holy Supper to their "prefigurations" (foreshadowings) in the Old Testament, starting with the offerings of Cain and Abel.</meta>

(110)
<meta>catchword: -vation (completing "salvation" from the previous page)</meta>

-vation, renewal, and reconciliation—although they enjoy these in different ways. 1 Corinthians 10:14–22; 1 Corinthians 11:23–30; Ephesians 1:10 and chapter 5:2. To understand this correctly, one must take another look into the Old Testament and therein perceive the prefigurations <term>prefigurations: historical events, persons, or rituals in the Old Testament that are seen as symbolic "types" or shadows of the New Testament's realities</term> of reconciliation, of Baptism, and of the Holy Supper somewhat more precisely than is commonly done. Thus, let a prefiguration of reconciliation be the offering of Abel and Cain (Genesis 4), with which all burnt offerings of the Old Testament agree (Genesis 22:2; Exodus 29; Leviticus 1 through chapter 8). These two brothers both came and offered before the Lord, each with his own property: Abel, who was a shepherd of livestock, with the firstlings of his flock; and Cain, who was a tiller of the land, with the fruits of the earth. God looked upon Abel’s offering with grace, for therein stood the image of Christ in Abel’s believing and hoping love, which through sharp imagination <note>In the mystical tradition of writers like Jacob Boehme, "imagination" is not "fantasy," but the spiritual faculty of the will that focuses and gives form to divine power</note> entered into the sacrifice by means of the holy magical fire <term>magical fire: a term used in 17th-century theosophy to describe the divine, spiritual fire of God's presence that consumes the earthly to reveal the spiritual</term>, which ignited the sacrifice as a testimony that it was acceptable (compare <note>original: "Conf." abbreviated from the Latin "confer"</note> Leviticus 9:23).

<summary>This page explains that the sacraments of the New Testament are foreshadowed by Old Testament sacrifices, specifically focusing on the offerings of Abel and Cain. It interprets Abel’s sacrifice as a mystical prefiguration of Christ’s love, ignited by divine spiritual fire.</summary>
<keywords>reconciliation, prefiguration, Abel and Cain, sacrifice, Baptism, Holy Supper, imagination, magical fire, typology</keywords>

This was because Cain did not focus his imagination <note>In this mystical context, "imagination" refers to the spiritual power of the soul to focus its will and "form" an inner reality</note> and inner vision into the offering, but rather more into the world and what is in the world, and had, as it were, lost himself therein. Thus, Cain stands here before us in the figure <note>original: *Figur*; a symbolic "type" or representative model of a certain kind of person</note> of corrupt man after the Fall, and Abel in the figure of the reborn man, who unites himself with GOTT through believing and hoping love; [GOTT] who has come in Christ to seek out corrupt and lost man, to give him new birth and to make him blessed.

### §. XVII. 

Just as natural food—bread and wine—demonstrates its internal effect through its spiritual and lively substances, not only in how it nourishes, strengthens, and gladdens a person, but specifically: 

(1) it resists all putrefaction and decay, dampens **cold gangrene** <term>original: *kalten Brand*; an early medical term for mortification or tissue death occurring without inflammation</term>, quenches thirst, and stills disordered appetite; 

(2) it awakens the forces of life and movement, refines <note>original: *subtilisiret*; in alchemy and early medicine, this meant to make a substance thinner or more "spirit-like" so it could flow better</note> the blood for its proper **circulation**, softens **strokes** <note>original: *den Schlag*; referring to apoplexy or sudden paralysis</note>, breaks **lethargy and the falling sickness** <term>original: *Schlaff-und hinfallende Sucht*; historical terms for coma/chronic drowsiness and epilepsy</term>, dissolves and disperses all phlegmatic and stubborn matter in the nerves, and thus removes the causes of **obstructions**; 

(3) it strengthens the brain and memory, makes one courageous and joyful, clears dimmed eyes and draws the heat out of them; 

externally, however, (4) it dissolves and disperses cold swellings and hard boils, and promotes the expulsion of the vapors and winds <note>original: *Bläste und Winde*; referring to "flatulence" or trapped gases within the bodily tissues</note> that dwell between the muscles; 

(5) it prevents the wasting of limbs and the clotting of blood in crushes and bruises, and prevents that which typically 

->follows<-

<sig>K</sig>

-sensitivity of the spirit in the will, and of the light in the intellect; a powerful, living, and fervent longing of the sanctified human will to unite itself with that toward which the enlightened human intellect—because of thoroughly recognized good, true, and certain circumstances—likewise inclines and leans, or to remain constant in that union. my only-begotten Son **Jesus**, your **Savior**, shall be your eternal **Mediator**, **Redeemer**, and **King**; and you, in turn, [shall be] his reconciled and redeemed members, brothers, sisters, **fellow citizens of the kingdom**, and **fellow heirs**. # Hymn.

Away! with all <term>vanity: the emptiness and transience of worldly life (originally "Eitelkeit")</term>/
->The spirit only for **God**<-
->longs:<-
Away with what time gives and <note>original: "un̅ ni̅mt" (und nimmt). The bar over the letters indicates a double "n" or "m", a common shorthand in older German scripts.</note> takes/
->With which the <term>worldling: a person focused on material rather than spiritual concerns (originally "Weltling")</term> boasts.<-
What is visible quickly passes away:

# Appendix.

Scarcely has the beautiful Subterranean Juno <note>Under-irdische Juno: Likely an alchemical or mythological personification of refined earth or mineral matter.</note> shown herself to the world with her celestial bridegroom Magniphosauro <note>A constructed name likely meaning "Great Light-Serpent," possibly representing a solar or phosphorous principle.</note> in her mysterious Hall of Wonders, when certain dreamers and irrational bunglers <term>Bönhaasen: Literally "attic-rabbits," a derogatory term for craftsmen who worked outside of the official guilds; here meaning unqualified critics.</term> have already been found who—notwithstanding the admonition and warning issued to the kind reader at the very beginning—have dared to carp at <note>original: "carpiren," from the Latin *carpere*, meaning to pluck at or find fault with.</note> or criticize the author. Their complaint is that he mixes and compares divine things with natural things, and draws conclusions from the latter about the former. Therefore, I, the author, have deemed it necessary to bring to the minds of these unhealthily rational critics and foolish simpletons, with appropriate gentleness, the manner in which they, through their untimely, irrational, and very tasteless judgment, insult not me, but **GOD** and His Eternal, Independent Word, which to me in this

<summary>In this appendix, the author defends his work against critics who believe he improperly mixes spiritual theology with natural philosophy. He uses allegorical figures like Juno and Magniphosauro to frame his response to those he considers "irrational bunglers."</summary>
<keywords>defense of work, allegory, Subterranean Juno, Magniphosauro, natural philosophy vs. theology, critics</keywords>

piece <note>original: "Stück"</note> has all-wisely shone forth, only to carp at and criticize it. For the all-wise **GOD** has revealed His greatest mysteries in and through natural things, and from them has drawn conclusions about Himself. Therefore, Master Know-it-all <note>original: "Meiſter Klügling," a sarcastic term for a pedantic or self-important critic.</note> go first and settle it with the **LORD** and His Word: that He, in Genesis 3:15, calls the Savior of the world the "Seed of the Woman"; that He placed the mystery of rebirth <term>Wiedergeburth: Often translated as regeneration; the spiritual renewal of a person.</term> into fleshly circumcision (Genesis 17:10 etc.); and laid the mystery of atonement into an animal (Exodus 12). He presented the Son from eternity in the form of a serpent (Numbers 21:9, compare <note>original: "Confer"</note> John 3:14). He turned away from men and spoke through lowlier creatures (Jeremiah 1 from the beginning to verse 7). He prefigured the essential heavenly body and blood of Christ with a glowing coal (Isaiah 6:6 etc.). Note also that the Eternal Wisdom of God in the flesh, **JESUS**, compared Himself to the light (John 12:35); the teaching and life of His disciples and apostles to salt (Matthew 4:16, Chapter 5:13, 16); human failings to a beam and a splinter <note>Balcken und Splitter: Usually translated in English Bibles as the "mote" and the "beam" or the "speck" and the "log."</note> (Matthew 7:5); the believing Canaanite woman to a dog; and Divine...

If these fault-finding bunglers <term>Bönhaasen: An old German term for "unlicensed artisans" or "clumsy workers." Here, the author uses it to insult his critics as theological amateurs who lack true insight.</term> did not let themselves be tied to the authority of the learned—like a donkey to a manger—and if they did not hold the reputation of men and distinguished people (singing that same old song <note>original: "mit der alten Leyer," literally "with the old lyre," an idiom meaning a tiresome, repetitive argument.</note>) higher than **GOD**, His prophets, Christ, and the Apostles, then I would have kindly asked them to take well to heart the magnificent discourse of King David composed in the 19th, 104th, and 139th Psalms.

the pious **GOD** has so laid and infused Himself and His holy essence into the creatures <term>Creaturen: From the Latin "creatura," referring to all created things—animals, plants, and elements—rather than just "monsters" or "animals" in the modern sense.</term> and clearly shown Himself therein, that one must be a foolish hypocrite <term>Tockmäuser: A derogatory term for someone who is willfully ignorant, a "cringer," or someone who hides from the truth; here implying a person spiritually blind to the obvious.</term> who cannot recognize and conclude—from the natural light and radiance—**GOD’S** uncreated light and radiance of joy; from the sweetness of honey, the pleasantness of the Creator; from the thunderstorm, His omnipotence; and from the air, the omnipresence of the **LORD** of heaven and earth, who made everything that exists, etc. Furthermore, let the valiant Catholic Escalante <note>Likely referring to a Spanish theologian; the title "Banus Creationis" may refer to "The Rule" or "Weight" of Creation.</note> step forward and hear what he reports in his book titled *Banus Creationis* or *The Weight of Creation*, Chapter 3, note 17:

Folio 67.

# Printing errors to be corrected as
# follows:

On the Title [page] line 12, it says **Schau-paltz** <note>Original: "Schau-paltz," meaning "Show-palace" or "Theater." The author is defending his use of an archaic spelling over the more modern "Schauplatz."</note>. Some might think that this is an error. However, **Paltz** is a good, old German word, and it means the same as a **Palace** or **Pallatz**, originating from the Latin word *Palatium* <note>Latin: Palace</note>. Modern-speaking Germans make use of this "misbegotten word" <term>Afftergeburth: Literally "afterbirth," used here as a derogatory term for a linguistic corruption or a debased form of a word.</term>, namely **Pfaltz** <note>The modern German "Pfalz," referring to a Palatinate or imperial palace.</note>, even though the Latin is *Palatium*, the **Paltz** or **Palace**; *Comes Palatinus* <note>Latin: Count Palatine</note> is the **Paltz-Graff** <note>Count of the Palace</note> and not *Comes Pfalatinus*, the **Pfaltz-Graff**.

Leaf 5, line 13, read: **Building**.
Leaf 10, line 15, read: **Mumia** <term>Mumia: In early modern science and alchemy, this referred to a preparation made from embalmed bodies, believed to have medicinal properties, or more broadly to the "vital balsam" of a living body.</term>.
Leaf 12, line 10, read: **Substance**.
line 12 at the end and line 13 at the beginning, read: **therefore**.
Leaf 16, line 6 at the beginning, read: **yet**.
line 9, read: **Change**.
Leaf 21, line 15, read: **incorporated**.
Leaf 25, line 14, read: **the snakes, monkeys, and elephants**.
Leaf 27, line 2, read: **constructed**.
Leaf 32, line 20, read: **Crystalline** <note>followed by a circle symbol with a horizontal bar, which in alchemy often represents Salt, one of the three primary primes of Paracelsian chemistry.</note>.
Leaf 44, line 8, read: **about**.
